SLV rises from 2835 to 2906 at Gen~1 ($+$2.5\\%) before declining to 2865 at Gen~3, while $r_{\\text{eff}}$ peaks one generation later at Gen~2 (580.9 vs.\\ 570.8 at Gen~1).\nThis one-generation SLV lead over $r_{\\text{eff}}$ (absent at 410M, where both peak simultaneously) suggests that SLV sensitivity increases with model scale.\nPerplexity rises monotonically throughout (8.1$\\to$13.4, $+$66\\%), confirming the downstream-spectral asymmetry at a second scale.\nFull 10-generation characterization at 1B remains future work.\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Mixing Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:phase1}\n\nWe evaluate the controller retrospectively by computing its signal on pre-existing fixed-ratio trajectories rather than deploying it in a closed loop; the goal is to characterize signal attenuation, not to propose operational deployment.\nUnder pure synthetic training, SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2468) and declines monotonically; with 50\\% mixing, SLV rises to 2572 at Gen~10, with only a negligible Gen~1 dip ($\\Delta_1 \\approx 0.0005$, well below $\\tau$).\nThe adaptive controller ($k{=}10$) remains silent through all ten generations.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:controller_signal} quantifies the signal attenuation: the maximum controller signal magnitude drops from 40.4 at $r{=}0$ to 3.8 at $r{=}0.2$ and reaches exactly zero at $r \\geq 0.3$, confirming that even moderate mixing suffices to suppress the rate-based signal~\\citep{scheffer2009ews}.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/controller_signal.pdf}\n\\caption{Maximum controller signal magnitude ($\\max|\\Delta\\tilde{S}_t|$, $t \\geq 2$) across mixing ratios. The signal drops $10\\times$ from $r{=}0$ to $r{=}0.2$ and is exactly zero for $r \\geq 0.3$ (``silent''), rendering rate-based controllers ineffective.}\n\\label{fig:controller_signal}\n\\end{figure}\n\nDespite sustained spectral expansion at $r{=}0.5$, downstream metrics degrade: perplexity increases 58\\% and distinct-4 falls to .152 (below even pure collapse), reinforcing that spectral health does not guarantee model health.\n\n\\subsection{Alternative EWS Comparison}\n\\label{subsec:alt_ews}\n\nWe compare SLV against seven alternative collapse indicators from three signal sources: embedding geometry (RankMe, isotropy), attention weights (stable rank, spectral norm), and output distributions (token entropy, raw perplexity, perplexity growth rate).\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:ews_main} summarizes the comparison under pure-synthetic training ($r{=}0$); full tabular data appear in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:ews_comparison}.\nOnly SLV and RankMe~\\citep{rankme2023} exhibit a decompression peak at Gen~2 followed by monotonic decline; all other indicators change monotonically from Gen~0 and cannot distinguish collapse onset from the initial fine-tuning transient.\nSLV's signal-to-noise ratio is 2.5$\\times$ that of RankMe (11.8\\% vs.\\ 4.7\\% relative change).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{4pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nIndicator & Direction ($r{=}0$) & Rel $\\Delta$ & Rev. \\\\\n\\midrule\nSLV         & Peak G2, mono.\\ $\\downarrow$ & 11.8\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nRankMe      & Peak G2, mono.\\ $\\downarrow$ &  4.7\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nToken ent.  & Mono.\\ $\\downarrow$           &  9.6\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nStable rank & Mono.\\ $\\downarrow$           & 14.0\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nSpec.\\ norm & Mono.\\ $\\uparrow$            &  8.0\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nIsotropy    & Non-mono.\\ (6 rev.)           & 75\\%   & \\checkmark \\\\\nRaw PPL     & Mono.\\ $\\uparrow$            & 704\\%  & $\\times$ \\\\\nPPL growth  & ${\\sim}$Mono.\\ $\\downarrow$   &  ---   & \\checkmark \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Eight EWS indicators under pure-synthetic training ($r{=}0$, Pythia-410M). \\textbf{Direction}: trajectory shape (Peak G$n$ = decompression peak at generation $n$; mono.\\ = monotonic post-peak or throughout). \\textbf{Rel $\\Delta$}: total relative change. \\textbf{Rev.}: whether the indicator reverses under $r{=}0.5$ mixing. Seven of eight reverse; raw PPL is the sole exception. Full data in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:ews_comparison}.}\n\\label{tab:ews_main}\n\\end{table}\nSeven of eight indicators reverse direction under mixing (Table~\\ref{tab:ews_main}, last column); raw perplexity is the sole exception.\nThis near-universality confirms that signal attenuation is not specific to SLV: any rate-based controller will fall silent when mixing exceeds $r_{\\text{crit}}$.\nSLV is the only indicator that combines decompression-phase sensitivity, post-peak monotonicity, and sub-second computation cost; it shares a metric foundation with SIGMA's $\\log\\det G$ ($= 2 \\times \\text{SLV}$; \\S\\ref{sec:related}).\n\n\\subsection{Collapse Trajectory Classification}\n\\label{subsec:phase_diagram_exp}\n\nWe now map SLV trajectories across mixing ratios to construct the collapse trajectory map defined in \\S\\ref{subsec:phase_diagram}.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:phase_diagram} plots SLV across 10 recursive generations for five mixing ratios; three qualitatively distinct trajectory types emerge:\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/phase_diagram.pdf}\n\\caption{SLV trajectories across mixing ratios (Pythia-410M; $r{=}0$, $r{=}0.2$, $r{=}0.25$, $r{=}0.3$: 3-seed mean, solid lines; $r{=}0.5$: 2-seed mean, dashed line). Type~I ($r{=}0$): decompression then monotonic collapse. Type~II ($r{=}0.2$, $r{=}0.25$): transient dip followed by recovery. Type~III ($r{=}0.3$, $r{=}0.5$): monotonic expansion from Gen~2 onward. The shaded band marks the critical threshold $r_{\\text{crit}} \\in (0.25, 0.3]$. The dashed horizontal line indicates the Gen~0 baseline.}\n\\label{fig:phase_diagram}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Type~I: Pure Collapse ($r{=}0$).}\nSLV exhibits large decompression ($+$96 points at Gen~1, peaking at Gen~2) followed by accelerating contraction (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}).\n\n\\paragraph{Type~II: Near-critical Mixing ($r{=}0.2$, $r{=}0.25$).}\nAt $r{=}0.2$, SLV dips $-7.3$ points at Gen~2 before recovering to 2467.2 at Gen~10 ($+4.3\\%$ above baseline; full per-generation data in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:pergen_variance_r02}).\nThe EMA-smoothed SLV violates non-decreasing at Gen~2, placing $r{=}0.2$ below $r_{\\text{crit}}$, yet the smoothed signal ($\\max \\Delta_t = 0.0016$, below $\\tau{=}0.002$) fails to trigger the controller.\nAt $r{=}0.25$, the same pattern holds with a weaker dip ($-2.1$) and ${\\sim}3\\times$ weaker signal.\nAs Figure~\\ref{fig:rankme_trajectories} shows, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ rises monotonically for all $r \\geq 0.2$, yielding only a 2-type classification (collapse vs.\\ non-collapse); SLV's transient dip uniquely identifies the transitional regime.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/rankme_trajectories.pdf}\n\\caption{Effective rank ($r_{\\mathrm{eff}}$) trajectories across mixing ratios. Unlike SLV (Figure~\\ref{fig:phase_diagram}), $r_{\\mathrm{eff}}$ rises monotonically for all $r \\geq 0.2$, providing only a 2-type classification and failing to identify the Type~II transitional regime.}\n\\label{fig:rankme_trajectories}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Type~III: Super-critical Mixing ($r \\geq 0.3$).}\nSLV rises monotonically from Gen~2 onward.\nAt $r{=}0.3$, SLV reaches 2508.2 at Gen~10 ($+$6.0\\% above baseline); at $r{=}0.5$, 2572 ($+$8.7\\%).\nThe critical threshold is robust to EMA smoothing ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.3, 0.5, 0.7\\}$ all yield $r_{\\text{crit}} \\in (0.25, 0.3]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:alpha_robustness}), and the maximum SLV departure below baseline (multi-seed mean) shrinks continuously with $r$ ($-7.3$ at $r{=}0.2$, $-3.3$ at $r{=}0.25$, $-1.5$ at $r{=}0.3$), consistent with a continuous crossover.\n$r_{\\text{crit}}$ thus marks a transition in trajectory shape, not in final outcome: Type~II trajectories recover spectrally but exhibit an initial contraction transient that Type~III trajectories lack.\n\n\\paragraph{Output Diversity Divergence.}\nD-4 does not increase monotonically with $r$: it remains highest around $r \\in \\{0.2, 0.25\\}$ (mean $.324$--$.342$, with high cross-seed variance s.d.\\ $\\approx 0.17$) before dropping to .152 at $r{=}0.5$, indicating that spectral expansion does not guarantee diversity.\nD-4 also shows substantially higher cross-seed variance than SLV (CV $= 3.9\\%$ vs.\\ $0.12\\%$ at $r{=}0.25$), confirming that the two metrics capture orthogonal aspects of model behavior.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:phase_summary} consolidates the final-generation metrics across all five mixing ratios.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{4pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}cccccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$r$ & Type & SLV & $r_{\\text{eff}}$ & PPL & D-4 & $\\max\\Delta_t$ & Gen. \\\\\n\\midrule\n0    & I   & 2181.4 & 438.1 & 71.0\\phantom{0} & .324 & ---   & 10 \\\\\n0.20 & II  & 2467.2 & 508.1 & 13.54 & .324 & .0016 & 10 \\\\\n0.25 & II  & 2488.1 & 513.8 & 13.41 & .342 & .0005 & 10 \\\\\n0.30 & III & 2508.2 & 521.1 & 13.34 & .295 & 0     & 10 \\\\\n0.50 & III & 2571.6 & 553.5 & 15.11 & .152 & 0     & 10 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Final-generation spectral and downstream metrics across mixing ratios (all runs to Gen~10). PPL = perplexity, D-4 = distinct-4. $\\max\\Delta_t$ computed for $t \\geq 2$, excluding the initial fine-tuning transient. Type classification follows \\S\\ref{subsec:phase_diagram}. $r{=}0$ reports the mean of three seeds (42, 43, 44); $r{=}0.3$ the mean of three seeds (42, 43, 44); $r{=}0.2$ the mean of three seeds (42, 43, 44); $r{=}0.5$ the mean of two seeds (42, 43); $r{=}0.25$ the mean of three seeds (42, 43, 44). D-4 exhibits high cross-seed variance at $r{=}0$ (s.d.\\ $= 0.15$) and $r{=}0.3$ (s.d.\\ $= 0.155$).}\n\\label{tab:phase_summary}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Practical Implications.}\nNeither second-order acceleration ($\\Delta^2_t$, Eq.~\\ref{eq:second_order}) nor level-based triggers resolve the attenuation problem: $\\Delta^2_t$ is negative in late generations regardless of $r$, and level-based triggers fail under Type~III.\nThe trajectory map provides a direct prescription: setting $r > 0.3$ prevents spectral collapse without per-generation monitoring.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{subsec:cross_arch}\n\nTo test whether the three-phase trajectory generalizes beyond Pythia, we repeat the recursive pipeline on GPT-2-medium~\\citep{radford2019language} (355M parameters, different pretraining corpus and tokenizer) under four conditions: pure synthetic ($r{=}0$, 9 generations), intermediate mixing ($r{=}0.25$ and $r{=}0.3$, 3 generations each), and a real-data control ($r{=}1.0$, 3 generations).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{4pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}cccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$r$ & Type & SLV$_0$ & SLV$_{\\text{end}}$ & $\\Delta$SLV & Match \\\\\n\\midrule\n0    & I    & 689  & 415\\,(G9)  & $-$39.8\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\n0.25 & ---  & 689  & 738\\,(G3)  & $+$7.0\\%  & $\\dagger$ \\\\\n0.30 & III  & 689  & 737\\,(G3)  & $+$6.9\\%  & \\checkmark \\\\\n1.0  & Ctrl & 689  & 813\\,(G3)  & $+$17.9\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium (355M, seed~42). \\textbf{Match}: whether the trajectory type matches the corresponding Pythia-410M classification. $\\dagger$\\,Three-generation horizon does not resolve the Type~II/III distinction at this ratio.}\n\\label{tab:cross_arch}\n\\end{table}\n\nUnder pure synthetic training, GPT-2-medium reproduces the three-phase pattern (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_arch}): SLV peaks at Gen~2 (924, $+$34.1\\% from baseline) and declines to 415 by Gen~9 ($-$55.1\\% from peak), while perplexity rises from 15.5 to 329 ($+$2028\\%).\nThe $\\sigma_1$ trajectory follows the same U-shape (6598$\\to$1191 during decompression, rising to ${\\sim}$1570 by Gen~9), confirming that dominant-direction reconcentration under falling effective rank is architecture-independent.\nThe collapse magnitude is substantially larger than Pythia's ($-$55.1\\% vs.\\ $-$11.6\\% SLV decline), reflecting GPT-2-medium's more anisotropic initial spectrum ($r_{\\text{eff}}{=}23$ vs.\\ Pythia's 418).\n\nThe real-data control ($r{=}1.0$) shows monotonic expansion with perplexity \\emph{decreasing} ($-$27.4\\%), which confirms that collapse is specific to recursive synthetic training.\n\nAt intermediate ratios ($r{=}0.25$, $r{=}0.3$), both conditions reproduce the decompression phase (Gen~0$\\to$1: SLV $+$7.5--8.5\\%, $r_{\\text{eff}}$ from 23 to 136--142, $\\sigma_1$ $-$80--82\\%) and stabilize through Gen~3, with EMA-smoothed SLV monotonically non-decreasing.\nAt $r{=}0.3$ this matches Pythia's Type~III classification.\nAt $r{=}0.25$, the three-generation window does not resolve the Type~II/III distinction, since Pythia's characteristic EMA dip appears only at Gen~5+; the trajectory is consistent with the Pythia-410M phase boundary but does not independently confirm $r_{\\text{crit}}$ for this architecture.\n",

```text
1	
2	The spectral decompression phase reflects fine-tuning's redistribution of variance: early recursive training compresses the dominant singular direction while inflating subordinate ones, resembling isotropy regularization~\citep{ethayarajh2019contextual} reversed by recursive amplification.
3	That downstream performance degrades during this expansion confirms these indicators are collapse detectors, not quality measures.
4	SLV and perplexity are complementary: perplexity rises monotonically from Gen~0; SLV provides mechanistic diagnosis by distinguishing volume contraction from distributional broadening.
5	
6	A key finding is the prevention--detectability tradeoff: real-data mixing reverses the spectral trajectory entirely, and the controller signal drops $10\times$ from $r{=}0$ to $r{=}0.2$ and vanishes at $r \geq 0.3$, leaving no regime where prevention and detection coexist.
7	Our analysis identifies SLV as a cheap spectral indicator, reveals a reproducible three-phase collapse trajectory, and exposes the signal attenuation that limits reactive controllers.
8	The trajectory map localizes $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$; setting $r > r_{\text{crit}}$ prevents collapse without per-generation monitoring, with cross-architecture support from GPT-2-medium and preliminary scale evidence from Pythia-1B.
9	
```

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	\paragraph{Scale and Scope.}
5	We validate at two model scales (Pythia-410M and a 1B pilot through Gen~3) with all spectral metrics computed from last-layer embeddings only; intermediate layers may exhibit different dynamics.
6	The 1B pilot confirms the decompression peak but is insufficient to characterize full acceleration or estimate $r_{\text{crit}}$ at that scale.
7	All fine-tuning uses a single domain (OpenWebText, English web text); spectral collapse dynamics may differ for code, multilingual, or domain-specific corpora.
8	
9	\paragraph{Temporal Horizon.}
10	Ten recursive generations may be shorter than production pipelines.
11	Type~II (transitional) trajectories may eventually revert to collapse at longer horizons, which would shift $r_{\text{crit}}$ upward.
12	The proportional controller uses a single gain $k$ without integral or derivative terms; a more sophisticated design might partially mitigate signal attenuation.
13	
14	\paragraph{Generalization.}
15	Signal attenuation may extend to implicit-loop settings where web corpora contain model-generated content, but validating this requires controlled experiments with web-scale data.
16	Our use of AI coding and writing assistants is disclosed in Appendix~\ref{app:ai-assistants}.
17	
```

      "content": "\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Scale and Scope.}\nWe validate at two model scales (Pythia-410M and a 1B pilot through Gen~3) with all spectral metrics computed from last-layer embeddings only; intermediate layers may exhibit different dynamics.\nThe 1B pilot confirms the decompression peak but is insufficient to characterize full acceleration or estimate $r_{\\text{crit}}$ at that scale.\nAll fine-tuning uses a single domain (OpenWebText, English web text); spectral collapse dynamics may differ for code, multilingual, or domain-specific corpora.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal Horizon.}\nTen recursive generations may be shorter than production pipelines.\nType~II (transitional) trajectories may eventually revert to collapse at longer horizons, which would shift $r_{\\text{crit}}$ upward.\nThe proportional controller uses a single gain $k$ without integral or derivative terms; a more sophisticated design might partially mitigate signal attenuation.\n\n\\paragraph{Generalization.}\nSignal attenuation may extend to implicit-loop settings where web corpora contain model-generated content, but validating this requires controlled experiments with web-scale data.\nOur use of AI coding and writing assistants is disclosed in Appendix~\\ref{app:ai-assistants}.\n",
      "numLines": 17,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 17
    }
  },
  "sourceToolAssistantUUID": "858a1603-5a33-4005-a50f-0e164f2f7619",

107	
108	\section{Training and Compute Details}
109	\label{app:training_details}
110	
111	Fine-tuning uses AdamW (lr $5 \times 10^{-5}$, weight decay 0.01, linear warmup 5\%, effective batch size 32) for one epoch per generation, with gradient clipping at max norm 1.0.
112	Synthetic text is generated with temperature 1.0, top-$k{=}50$, max length 512.
113	All experiments run on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\,GB); each 10-generation pipeline requires 15--26 single-GPU hours depending on the mixing ratio, totaling approximately 220 GPU-hours across all reported experiments.
114	
115	\section{Use of AI Assistants}
116	\label{app:ai-assistants}
117	
118	We used Claude (Anthropic) as an AI coding and writing assistant
119	throughout this work. Specifically:
120	\begin{itemize}
121	    \item \textbf{Code:} drafting experiment scripts, data-processing
122	    pipelines, and analysis utilities; debugging and refactoring.
123	    \item \textbf{Writing:} drafting and revising paper sections,
124	    polishing prose, and reformatting \LaTeX{}.
125	    \item \textbf{Literature:} summarizing related papers to inform
126	    the related-work section.
127	\end{itemize}

The critical threshold $r_{\\text{crit}} \\in (0.25, 0.3]$ is invariant to $\\alpha$: signal is exactly zero for $r \\geq 0.3$ at all three smoothing values, confirming that the phase boundary is a structural property of the trajectory rather than an artifact of smoothing.}\n\\label{tab:alpha_robustness}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Training and Compute Details}\n\\label{app:training_details}\n\nFine-tuning uses AdamW (lr $5 \\times 10^{-5}$, weight decay 0.01, linear warmup 5\\%, effective batch size 32) for one epoch per generation, with gradient clipping at max norm 1.0.\nSynthetic text is generated with temperature 1.0, top-$k{=}50$, max length 512.\nAll experiments run on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB); each 10-generation pipeline requires 15--26 single-GPU hours depending on the mixing ratio, totaling approximately 220 GPU-hours across all reported experiments.\n\n\\section{Use of AI Assistants}\n\\label{app:ai-assistants}\n\nWe used Claude (Anthropic) as an AI coding and writing assistant\nthroughout this work. Specifically:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Code:} drafting experiment scripts, data-processing\n    pipelines, and analysis utilities; debugging and refactoring.\n    \\item \\textbf{Writing:} drafting and revising paper sections,\n    polishing prose, and reformatting \\LaTeX{}.\n    \\item \\textbf{Literature:} summarizing related papers to inform\n    the related-work section.\n\\end{itemize}\n",
      "numLines": 128,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 128
    }
  },

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/related_work.tex:15:\citet{he2025spectral} linked spectral collapse to plasticity loss in continual learning, and \citet{naderi2025mindgap} identified rank collapse in attention matrices.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/related_work.tex:18:Table~\ref{tab:related_comparison} summarizes the positioning.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/related_work.tex:45:\paragraph{Data Mixing for LLM Training.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:8:These three metrics (language modeling capability, natural language understanding, and generation diversity) cover the primary evaluation dimensions accessible to a 410M-parameter model.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:36:\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/slv_ppl_decoupling.pdf}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:37:\caption{SLV (top) and perplexity (bottom) across 10 recursive generations ($r{=}0$, 3-seed mean). In Phase~I (Gen~0--2), both metrics rise simultaneously. The SLV increase reflects distributional broadening, not model health. From Phase~III onward (Gen~4--10), the two metrics diverge: SLV declines monotonically while perplexity accelerates, which confirms that post-decompression SLV faithfully tracks structural collapse.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:62:Under pure synthetic training, SLV peaks at Gen~2 (2468) and declines monotonically; with 50\% mixing, SLV rises to 2572 at Gen~10, with only a negligible Gen~1 dip ($\Delta_1 \approx 0.0005$, well below $\tau$).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:102:\caption{Eight EWS indicators under pure-synthetic training ($r{=}0$, Pythia-410M). \textbf{Direction}: trajectory shape (Peak G$n$ = decompression peak at generation $n$; mono.\ = monotonic post-peak or throughout). \textbf{Rel $\Delta$}: total relative change. \textbf{Rev.}: whether the indicator reverses under $r{=}0.5$ mixing. Seven of eight reverse; raw PPL is the sole exception. Full data in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:ews_comparison}.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:171:The trajectory map provides a direct prescription: setting $r > 0.3$ prevents spectral collapse without per-generation monitoring.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:196:Under pure synthetic training, GPT-2-medium reproduces the three-phase pattern (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch}): SLV peaks at Gen~2 (924, $+$34.1\% from baseline) and declines to 415 by Gen~9 ($-$55.1\% from peak), while perplexity rises from 15.5 to 329 ($+$2028\%).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:200:The real-data control ($r{=}1.0$) shows monotonic expansion with perplexity \emph{decreasing} ($-$27.4\%), which confirms that collapse is specific to recursive synthetic training.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:202:At intermediate ratios ($r{=}0.25$, $r{=}0.3$), both conditions reproduce the decompression phase (Gen~0$\to$1: SLV $+$7.5--8.5\%, $r_{\text{eff}}$ from 23 to 136--142, $\sigma_1$ $-$80--82\%) and stabilize through Gen~3, with EMA-smoothed SLV monotonically non-decreasing.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/conclusion.tex:4:SLV and perplexity are complementary: perplexity rises monotonically from Gen~0; SLV provides mechanistic diagnosis by distinguishing volume contraction from distributional broadening.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/conclusion.tex:8:The trajectory map localizes $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$; setting $r > r_{\text{crit}}$ prevents collapse without per-generation monitoring, with cross-architecture support from GPT-2-medium and preliminary scale evidence from Pythia-1B.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/method.tex:74:A sustained negative $\Delta^2_t$ indicates decelerating SLV growth, a potential precursor to stagnation even when SLV is still rising.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/abstract.tex:1:During the early stages of recursive synthetic training, spectral indicators of the embedding space suggest the model is improving, even as downstream performance already degrades.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/abstract.tex:7:Setting the mixing ratio above the calibrated $r_{\text{crit}}$ prevents collapse without real-time monitoring, reducing the problem to offline calibration.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/appendix.tex:39:\caption{Per-generation metrics under pure-synthetic training ($r{=}0$, Pythia-410M, mean$_{\pm\text{s.d.}}$ across seeds 42, 43, 44). Gen~0 shares a single pretrained checkpoint; D-4 variance at Gen~0 reflects generation sampling. Bold marks the spectral peak.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/appendix.tex:66:\caption{Per-generation metrics under transitional mixing ($r{=}0.2$, Pythia-410M, mean$_{\pm\text{s.d.}}$ across seeds 42, 43, 44). Gen~0 shares a single pretrained checkpoint; D-4 variance at Gen~0 reflects generation sampling. HellaSwag was not evaluated for mixing experiments.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/appendix.tex:104:\caption{Normalized maximum controller signal magnitude ($\max|\Delta\tilde{S}_t| / \tilde{S}_t$, $t \geq 2$) across mixing ratios and EMA smoothing parameters. The critical threshold $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$ is invariant to $\alpha$: signal is exactly zero for $r \geq 0.3$ at all three smoothing values, confirming that the phase boundary is a structural property of the trajectory rather than an artifact of smoothing.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/appendix.tex:122:    pipelines, and analysis utilities; debugging and refactoring.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:2:Synthetic data has become integral to language model training.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:3:Instruction tuning, knowledge distillation, and data augmentation all routinely use model-generated text, and as synthetic corpora are recycled into training pipelines, the process often becomes recursive: each generation of models is fine-tuned on outputs from the previous one.

Let me now check for the triple-dash "---" in figure captions (em-dash in LaTeX format). The `---` in the related_work.tex caption on line 4 is an em-dash rendered via LaTeX. Let me check all instances.

Let me also check:
1. Contribution-scope alignment
2. Pages count
3. Check for `\hline` usage
4. Check for `significantly` claims with statistical backing
5. Check the abstract more carefully for the "four questions" structure

Let me now do the comprehensive audit systematically:

### 1. Narrative Consistency (CRITICAL)


The abstract frames the core problem as misleading spectral signals during recursive synthetic training—the model appears to improve according to spectral geometry while downstream performance actually degrades. The introduction reinforces this same issue and identifies the key gaps: spectral geometry is poorly understood, there's no clear connection between spectral indicators and when downstream degradation occurs, and there's no framework to prevent collapse. The contributions then address this directly with a multi-seed characterization across ten generations that reveals a three-phase pattern, plus SLV as a cheap collapse indicator that shows monotonic decline after its peak and correlates with HellaSwag performance.

The third contribution identifies signal attenuation under mixing—the controller signal degrades and eventually vanishes. The method itself defines the spectral metrics, designs an adaptive mixing controller, and classifies collapse trajectories. The experiments systematically verify each contribution: the three-phase pattern appears in section 4.2, the adaptive mixing comparison demonstrates signal attenuation in 4.3, alternative early warning systems are compared in 4.4, collapse trajectory classification yields a critical radius in 4.5, and cross-architecture validation confirms the findings hold more broadly. The conclusion then frames the decompression phase as a form of fine-tuning.

Now I'm checking the abstract against the experimental results to verify consistency. The abstract mentions eight candidate indicators—the experiments compare SLV against seven others, which checks out. The HellaSwag drop of 3.3 percentage points matches what's shown in the table. The SLV decline from peak to the final value calculates to exactly 11.6%. I'm verifying the remaining claims about the five levels... of mixing ratio - Experiments has r ∈ {0, 0.2, 0.25, 0.3, 0.5} = 5 levels ✓
5. Abstract says "$r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$" - matches experiments ✓
6. Abstract says "GPT-2-medium (355M)" - matches cross-arch section ✓

Now let me check if C2 is adequately supported. C2 claims "HellaSwag-spectral asymmetry: downstream degradation begins during the spectral decompression phase." The experiments do show this in §4.2 Phase I. ✓

But wait - C2 says "monotonic post-peak decline." In §4.4 (Alt EWS Comparison), Table 3 shows SLV has "Peak G2, mono. ↓" - confirming post-peak monotonicity. ✓

Checking for narrative drift... The intro frames four gaps:
1. No characterization of spectral dynamics beyond a few generations → C1 addresses this
2. Temporal relationship between spectral indicators and downstream → C2 addresses this  
3. No connection to closed-loop collapse prevention via EWS → C3 addresses this
4. Relationship between mixing ratios and spectral collapse not mapped → Supporting finding (trajectory map)

This is well-structured. But there's a subtle issue: Gap 3 mentions "closed-loop collapse prevention through the lens of early warning signals (EWS)" - the paper's actual finding is that EWS-based controllers DON'T work (signal attenuation). This is actually well-framed as a negative/insight contribution, which strengthens rather than weakens the narrative.

There's a real tension in the framing though: the title promises "Spectral Early Warning Signals for Model Collapse" but the core finding is that these signals fail in reactive closed-loop settings due to attenuation. The paper does resolve this by positioning the trajectory map as the practical alternative, but a reviewer might expect the title to reflect that EWS have fundamental limitations rather than suggesting they're a working solution.

Looking at the abstract, it covers the key elements well—the problem of misleading spectral signals, the method of tracking spectral properties across generations, the three-phase pattern and SLV effectiveness, and the practical payoff of offline calibration replacing real-time monitoring. Word count is solid at around 191 words, well under the EMNLP limit.

For the introduction, I'm verifying the structure flows logically: it opens with synthetic data and recursive training leading to collapse, then identifies the gap that spectral geometry remains poorly understood despite collapse being well-characterized. The current detection methods rely on expensive downstream metrics, which I'm listing as four specific gaps. Then I'm laying out our approach—tracking spectral indicators to reveal the three-phase pattern—followed by the three main contributions plus supporting results. The intro also includes Figure 1 to ground the narrative, and this overall arc feels solid.

Now I'm checking that the related work is organized thematically rather than as a list of who-did-what, covering model collapse, spectral analysis, early warning systems and critical transitions, and data mixing. There's a positioning table that compares our work to prior approaches, which sits naturally in that section.

I'm also mapping each contribution back to where it appears in the results: C1 connects to the spectral collapse characterization and Table 1, C2 spans the phase analysis and alternative early warning signals with Table 3, and C3 covers the adaptive mixing strategy and trajectory classification. The supporting findings like the trajectory map and cross-architecture validation are accounted for in the corresponding sections and figures.

Looking at the methods sections, the spectral metrics, adaptive mixing controller, and trajectory classification all serve clear purposes in supporting the main contributions. The alternative EWS comparison in §4.4 is doing significant work to validate C2, even though it's not explicitly called out as its own contribution—it's woven into the evidence for why SLV is effective. There's also an interesting diversity divergence finding at the end of §4.5 that's somewhat tangential to the stated contributions, but overall the structure feels cohesive without orphaned sections.

Now checking the abstract and title against what's actually in the experiments—the three seeds, five mixing levels, and GPT-2-medium cross-architecture tests all check out. For claim strength, I'm scanning for overstatements and finding mostly measured language. The paper cites support for "data composition significantly affects LM quality" and avoids absolute claims like "state-of-the-art" or "novel." The main claims about SLV being the most effective collapse indicator and showing monotonic decline are backed by the comparison table, though I should verify the monotonic decline claim is fully supported. supported by data showing strict monotonic decline
3. "reproducible" - supported by 3-seed replication with CV < 1.3%
4. "robust to threshold reduction" (C3) - supported by the τ-independence argument
5. "robust to both the EMA smoothing parameter and random seed" - supported by Appendix Table 5

The claims seem well-calibrated. No overclaiming detected.

The intro mentions "SLV as a computationally cheap collapse indicator with monotonic post-peak decline" — and while <1s might seem debatable, it's clearly cheap relative to the 70-minute benchmark runtime, and the comparison is quantified. The forward reference in §4.2 to the Alt EWS comparison in §4.4 is implicit but not problematic since both are within the Experiments section.

Now looking at experimental rigor: the paper reports standard deviations in Table 1's caption and full per-generation statistics in Appendix Table 2, with cross-seed CV under 1.3%, though Table 1 itself lacks inline error bars. Table 4 shows seed counts per condition but also no inline uncertainty measures. The baseline comparisons are starting to be laid out but the thought cuts off there. Looking at the reproducibility details, the paper provides solid coverage on hyperparameters, hardware specs, and training time, with code availability through an anonymous GitHub link. The data preprocessing is somewhat underspecified regarding how the 50K OpenWebText subset was selected, and the limitations section spans three paragraphs addressing scale and other constraints.

Now checking the symbol and terminology consistency—SLV is properly defined early and used throughout, along with the effective rank notation and mixing ratio variables, all introduced clearly in the relevant sections. Checking the notation consistency for D-4 and other abbreviations—D-4 is introduced in table captions as shorthand for "distinct-4" while the text uses both forms interchangeably, which is a minor inconsistency but acceptable. HeSw and PPL follow similar patterns where they're defined in captions and used in tables, though the running text tends to spell out the full terms.

One concern is the abstract mentioning "eight candidate indicators" without defining what they are until much later in §4.4—readers might be left wondering initially, though abstracts typically prioritize brevity over completeness.

Looking at how the sections connect: the introduction flows naturally into related work through the positioning of gaps, though the transition from related work to methods could be smoother since it relies on an implicit section break rather than explicit bridging language. The method to experiments transition works well, with trajectory classification defined in one section and its experimental setup described in the next.

I'm noticing something odd about the paper structure—the pipeline overview figure appears in the related work section, which is unconventional. This figure typically belongs in the methods section or at the end of the introduction, so its placement here might be a LaTeX float positioning artifact rather than an intentional choice. The conclusion section combines discussion and interpretation, starting with how the decompression phase relates to isotropy regularization. I found a LaTeX em-dash in the figure caption that will render correctly as a typographic dash in the PDF. Now I'm systematically checking all triple-dash occurrences and looking more carefully at trailing "-ing" patterns to see if they match the problematic style the guidelines flag. I should also verify the "significantly" usage isn't overused and scan for any internal experiment references that shouldn't be there.

For the EMNLP 2026 submission requirements, the abstract is within the 200-word limit, the limitations section is properly placed in the main text rather than the appendix, and there's no ARR checklist included. The main concern is the page count—Phase 1 shows 11 pages total, but I need to determine whether that includes references and the limitations section, since those don't count toward the 8-page main text limit. ```text
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:30:\caption{Cross-seed reproducibility of SLV under pure-synthetic training ($r{=}0$). Thin lines: individual seeds (42, 43, 44); thick line: 3-seed mean; shaded band: $\pm$1 s.d. The coefficient of variation remains below 1.3\% at all generations, confirming that spectral collapse follows a deterministic trajectory.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:55:Perplexity rises monotonically throughout (8.1$\to$13.4, $+$66\%), confirming the downstream-spectral asymmetry at a second scale.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:64:Figure~\ref{fig:controller_signal} quantifies the signal attenuation: the maximum controller signal magnitude drops from 40.4 at $r{=}0$ to 3.8 at $r{=}0.2$ and reaches exactly zero at $r \geq 0.3$, confirming that even moderate mixing suffices to suppress the rate-based signal~\citep{scheffer2009ews}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:69:\caption{Maximum controller signal magnitude ($\max|\Delta\tilde{S}_t|$, $t \geq 2$) across mixing ratios. The signal drops $10\times$ from $r{=}0$ to $r{=}0.2$ and is exactly zero for $r \geq 0.3$ (``silent''), rendering rate-based controllers ineffective.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:145:D-4 does not increase monotonically with $r$: it remains highest around $r \in \{0.2, 0.25\}$ (mean $.324$--$.342$, with high cross-seed variance s.d.\ $\approx 0.17$) before dropping to .152 at $r{=}0.5$, indicating that spectral expansion does not guarantee diversity.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:146:D-4 also shows substantially higher cross-seed variance than SLV (CV $= 3.9\%$ vs.\ $0.12\%$ at $r{=}0.25$), confirming that the two metrics capture orthogonal aspects of model behavior.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:197:The $\sigma_1$ trajectory follows the same U-shape (6598$\to$1191 during decompression, rising to ${\sim}$1570 by Gen~9), confirming that dominant-direction reconcentration under falling effective rank is architecture-independent.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/abstract.tex:6:A proportional controller reveals inherent signal attenuation: mixing prevents the contraction that triggers the controller, rendering reactive feedback ineffective when prevention succeeds.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/appendix.tex:33:Spectral metrics ($r_{\text{eff}}$, SLV, $\sigma_1$) exhibit low cross-seed variance throughout the trajectory (SLV coefficient of variation $< 1.3\%$ at all generations), confirming that spectral collapse follows a deterministic geometric trajectory.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/appendix.tex:104:\caption{Normalized maximum controller signal magnitude ($\max|\Delta\tilde{S}_t| / \tilde{S}_t$, $t \geq 2$) across mixing ratios and EMA smoothing parameters. The critical threshold $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$ is invariant to $\alpha$: signal is exactly zero for $r \geq 0.3$ at all three smoothing values, confirming that the phase boundary is a structural property of the trajectory rather than an artifact of smoothing.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:22:We further design a proportional controller as a testbed for reactive collapse prevention, and find that it exposes an inherent signal attenuation: the rate-based spectral signal degrades continuously as the real-data fraction increases and vanishes entirely above $r_{\text{crit}}$, rendering any reactive spectral controller ineffective in the regime where prevention succeeds.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:26:\item \textbf{C1:} The first multi-seed spectral-downstream characterization over 10 recursive generations, revealing dynamics invisible in prior short-horizon studies and establishing a reproducible three-phase collapse pattern (decompression, onset, acceleration) across 10 generations (\S\ref{subsec:phase0}, Table~\ref{tab:phase0}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:27:\item \textbf{C2:} SLV as a computationally cheap collapse indicator with monotonic post-peak decline and a HellaSwag-spectral asymmetry: downstream degradation begins during the spectral decompression phase, demonstrating that spectral expansion does not indicate model health (\S\ref{subsec:phase0}).
```
